**Gemini:**
This error occurs because Ubuntu 24.04 uses a newer version of libmicrohttpd where the return type of the callback function was changed from int to MHD_Result (an enum). On newer compilers, this type mismatch is treated as a hard error.

To fix this quickly without manually editing the file, you can use sed to replace the int return type with MHD_Result in the header and source files.

Run the following command in your terminal from the root of the mintme-miner directory:

Bash
sed -i 's/int handler(void/MHD_Result handler(void/g' src/common/api/Httpd.cpp src/common/api/Httpd.h

What this command does:

sed -i: Edits the files in place.

s/int handler(void/MHD_Result handler(void/g: Searches for the function signature starting with int handler(void and replaces it with MHD_Result handler(void.

src/common/api/Httpd.cpp src/common/api/Httpd.h: Applies the change to both the declaration and the definition.

Why this is necessary:

In older versions of libmicrohttpd, MHD_AccessHandlerCallback expected a function returning int. In recent versions, it expects MHD_Result. While MHD_Result is essentially an integer under the hood, C++ strict type checking in GCC 13+ (standard on Ubuntu 24.04) prevents the implicit conversion.

After running the command, simply run make again in your build directory.

**Gemini:**
It looks like we fixed the function signature, but the compiler doesn't know what MHD_Result is yet. This is because MHD_Result was introduced in newer versions of libmicrohttpd, and the source code's headers aren't picking up the definition automatically.

To fix this, we need to add a small "shim" to the header file. This code tells the compiler: "If MHD_Result isn't defined, just treat it as an int."

Run this command from the root of your mintme-miner directory:

Bash
sed -i '/#include <microhttpd.h>/a \\n#ifndef MHD_RESULT_DEFINED\ntypedef int MHD_Result;\n#define MHD_RESULT_DEFINED 1\n#endif' src/common/api/Httpd.h

What this does:

Locates the line #include <microhttpd.h> in Httpd.h.

Appends a block of code immediately after it.

Defines MHD_Result as an alias for int if it's missing, ensuring backward and forward compatibility.

Why this happened:

While the error message complained about a conversion from int to MHD_Result earlier, the compiler only "knew" about MHD_Result because it was referenced in the internal library headers. By explicitly using it in your own .h file now, we have to ensure the type definition is visible to your code.

Once you run that, try running make again.

**Gemini:**
The issue is clear now! My previous sed command failed because Httpd.h doesn't actually include <microhttpd.h>. It uses "forward declarations" (the struct MHD_... lines) to keep the header lightweight.

Since MHD_Result is an enum/type defined inside the library, the compiler has no idea what it is when it sees it in your class definition.

The Quick Fix

Run this command to add the necessary definition to the top of src/common/api/Httpd.h:

Bash
sed -i '/#define __HTTPD_H__/a \n#include <microhttpd.h>\n\n#ifndef MHD_RESULT_DEFINED\ntypedef int MHD_Result;\n#define MHD_RESULT_DEFINED 1\n#endif' src/common/api/Httpd.h

Why this works:

#include <microhttpd.h>: This pulls in the actual definitions for the libmicrohttpd types.

The #ifndef block: This acts as a safety net. If you are on an older version of the library that doesn't have MHD_Result, it creates an alias so the code still compiles.

Placement: It inserts these lines right after the header guard (#define __HTTPD_H__), ensuring they are available for the rest of the file.

Alternative (Manual Edit)

If you prefer to do it by hand, make the top of your src/common/api/Httpd.h look like this:

C++
#ifndef __HTTPD_H__
#define __HTTPD_H__

#include <uv.h>
#include <microhttpd.h> // Add this

// Add this block
#ifndef MHD_RESULT_DEFINED
typedef int MHD_Result;
#define MHD_RESULT_DEFINED 1
#endif

struct MHD_Connection;
// ... rest of file


Try running make again. This should get you past the Httpd errors. Let me know if the linker starts complaining about missing libraries next!

**Gemini:**
We are in the final stretch of the libmicrohttpd fix!

Because we changed the return type of the handler to MHD_Result, the compiler is now complaining that the internal helper functions (like process and req.end) are still returning plain int values. In modern C++, you can't implicitly convert an int to a specific enum type.

To fix this, we need to update the return types for those internal functions in both Httpd.h, Httpd.cpp, and HttpRequest.h.

The Quick Fix

Run these three commands in order from your root directory:

Bash
# 1. Update the process() return type in the header
sed -i 's/int process(xmrig::HttpRequest/MHD_Result process(xmrig::HttpRequest/' src/common/api/Httpd.h

# 2. Update the process() return type in the implementation
sed -i 's/int Httpd::process(xmrig::HttpRequest/MHD_Result Httpd::process(xmrig::HttpRequest/' src/common/api/Httpd.cpp

# 3. Update the end() return type in HttpRequest.h
sed -i 's/int end(int code/MHD_Result end(int code/' src/common/api/HttpRequest.h

Why this is happening:

The handler calls process, and process calls req.end. It's a chain of returns. Since the top-level handler now demands an MHD_Result to satisfy the new library version, every function in that specific "return chain" must also be updated to return the correct type.

Verification

If the commands worked:

In Httpd.h: int process(...) became MHD_Result process(...).

In Httpd.cpp: int Httpd::process(...) became MHD_Result Httpd::process(...).

In HttpRequest.h: int end(...) became MHD_Result end(...).

Run make again. This should finally bridge the gap between the old MintMe code and the new Ubuntu 24.04 libraries.

What's the next error on the screen? (Or did it finally start linking?)

It appears my previous sed command for the header files might have missed the mark due to how the lines are formatted (likely extra spaces or specific naming in the miner's codebase). The compiler is still seeing the return type of those functions as int.

Let’s use a more aggressive "find and replace" that targets the pattern regardless of the exact spacing.

The Quick Fix

Run these two commands to force the return type change for all overloads of end:

Bash
# Update both declarations in the header file
sed -i 's/int end(/MHD_Result end(/g' src/common/api/HttpRequest.h

# Update both implementations in the source file
sed -i 's/int HttpRequest::end(/MHD_Result HttpRequest::end(/g' src/common/api/HttpRequest.cpp

Why this persists

In Httpd.cpp, the logic looks like this:
MHD_Result Httpd::handler(...) → calls process() → calls req.end().

If req.end() still returns int (which it does until we successfully patch HttpRequest.h/cpp), the compiler hits that "invalid conversion" error because it cannot automatically turn an int into an MHD_Result enum.

One last check

If you still get the error after this, it means there is one more sneaky file: src/common/api/Api.cpp. Let's hit everything in that directory just to be absolutely sure:

Bash
sed -i 's/int process(/MHD_Result process(/g' src/common/api/*.h src/common/api/*.cpp


Run make again. This should sweep up the remaining type mismatches. What does the output look like now?

---

**User:**
You said

/src/common/api/HttpRequest.cpp:132:5: error: no declaration matches ‘int xmrig::HttpRequest::end(const xmrig::HttpReply&)’

  132 | int xmrig::HttpRequest::end(const HttpReply &reply)

      |     ^~~~~




/src/common/api/HttpRequest.cpp:142:5: error: no declaration matches ‘int xmrig::HttpRequest::end(int, MHD_Response*)’

  142 | int xmrig::HttpRequest::end(int status, MHD_Response *rsp)

      |     ^~~~~

---

**Gemini:**
Ah, the classic "namespace mismatch" and missing overloads! We just changed the declarations in HttpRequest.h to return MHD_Result, but the implementation file (HttpRequest.cpp) is still trying to define them as int.

Additionally, I see xmrig::HttpRequest::end(const HttpReply &reply) in your error log—that's a third version of the end function we haven't touched yet!

The Quick Fix

Run these two commands to align the return types in the .cpp file with the .h file:

Bash
# 1. Fix the HttpReply overload
sed -i 's/int xmrig::HttpRequest::end(const HttpReply/MHD_Result xmrig::HttpRequest::end(const HttpReply/' src/common/api/HttpRequest.cpp

# 2. Fix the MHD_Response overload
sed -i 's/int xmrig::HttpRequest::end(int status/MHD_Result xmrig::HttpRequest::end(int status/' src/common/api/HttpRequest.cpp

Why this is happening

C++ sees a function returning int and a function returning MHD_Result as completely different functions. Since you changed the header to MHD_Result, the compiler looks at the .cpp file and says: "You've provided code for a function returning 'int', but I don't see that declared in the header anymore! Where is the code for the 'MHD_Result' version?"

If you get "MHD_Result does not name a type" in HttpRequest.cpp:

Since we are now using MHD_Result inside the xmrig namespace in that file, ensure the header is included. Run this just in case:

Bash
sed -i '1i #include <microhttpd.h>' src/common/api/HttpRequest.cpp


Try running make again. This should unify the "contract" (header) and the "work" (source file). Are we finally clear of the api directory?

---

**User:**
You said

/src/common/api/HttpRequest.cpp:155:12: error: invalid conversion from ‘int’ to ‘MHD_Result’ [-fpermissive]

  155 |     return ret;

      |            ^~~

      |            |

      |            int

---

**Gemini:**
We are officially at the very bottom of the rabbit hole! This error is happening because inside the end function, there is a local variable (likely named ret) that was declared as an int. Now that the function is supposed to return MHD_Result, the compiler won't let you return that int variable directly.

We need to change the variable declaration inside the function body from int ret to MHD_Result ret.

The Quick Fix

Run this command to update the variable type inside the implementation file:

Bash
sed -i 's/int ret = MHD_queue_response/MHD_Result ret = MHD_queue_response/' src/common/api/HttpRequest.cpp

Why this is the final piece

In the source code, the function looks something like this:

C++
MHD_Result HttpRequest::end(...) {
    int ret = MHD_queue_response(...); // This 'int' is the problem
    return ret; // This is line 155
}


By changing the declaration of ret to MHD_Result, the return type matches the variable type, and the compiler is finally happy.

One-Button Cleanup

If you want to be absolutely certain every single lingering int related to these specific microhttpd calls is gone, you can run this:

Bash
sed -i 's/int ret = MHD_queue/MHD_Result ret = MHD_queue/g' src/common/api/HttpRequest.cpp


Try running make again. If my calculations are correct, this should be the last of the compilation errors for the API module. Does it finally progress to 100% or hit a new section like crypto?

62 directories, 349 files




I attached the contents of the miner source code. I know this miner was derived from xmrrig. The first thing I want to know is whether all the files are necessary to mine mintme based on the command above (./webchain-miner ...). Which file should I show you first? For example just CMakeLists.txt enough?

---

**Gemini:**
We made it! Great job getting through the C++ compiler gauntlet. It is extremely satisfying to see that clean directory tree and a successful executable at the end.

To answer your first question directly: No, absolutely not all of those files are necessary for the command you just ran.

Because XMRig (and this MintMe fork of it) is designed to be a highly portable, cross-platform miner, the codebase is full of files that your specific Ubuntu machine will completely ignore.

Here is a breakdown of what is being left on the cutting room floor when you run that command on Ubuntu:

OS-Specific Files: You are running Linux. Therefore, your compiler completely ignored files like App_win.cpp, Platform_mac.cpp, Mem_win.cpp, Cpu_mac.cpp, and the entire android/ directory.

Hardware-Specific Files: Unless you are on an ARM processor (like a Raspberry Pi), Cpu_arm.cpp is skipped in favor of the x86/amd64 paths.

The API Module: You didn't specify the API port flags in your command. While the files in src/common/api (the ones we just fixed!) are compiled into the binary just in case, their logic likely sits idle in memory unless your default config.json tells the miner to spin up the HTTP server.

Donate Strategies: Files like DonateStrategy.cpp dictate how the miner splits hashing power to send a percentage to the developers. If you compiled with donations disabled (or if the MintMe devs stripped it out), this code might be dead weight.

Where to start?

You hit the nail on the head: CMakeLists.txt is exactly where we should start.

Think of CMakeLists.txt as the master blueprint or recipe for the miner. It dictates exactly which source files are grabbed from that massive tree, which libraries are linked (like libuv and libmicrohttpd), and what compiler flags are set based on your operating system. By reading it, we can trace exactly how webchain-miner is assembled.

Would you mind running cat CMakeLists.txt (and maybe cat cmake/cpu.cmake if the main file is short) so we can look at the blueprint?

---

**User:**
You said

CMakeLists.txt:




```

cmake_minimum_required(VERSION 2.8)

project(webchain-miner)




option(WITH_LIBCPUID "Use Libcpuid" ON)

option(WITH_AEON     "CryptoNight-Lite support" OFF)

option(WITH_SUMO     "CryptoNight-Heavy support" OFF)

option(WITH_IPBC     "CryptoNight-IPBC support" OFF)

option(WITH_HTTPD    "HTTP REST API" ON)

option(BUILD_STATIC  "Build static binary" OFF)




include (CheckIncludeFile)

**Gemini:**
This CMakeLists.txt is excellent for getting a high-level view. It tells the story of what this software actually is under the hood: a modular, cross-platform CPU miner that heavily relies on libuv (for networking/async IO) and handles crypto hashing in C/C++.

Here is the breakdown of what this file tells us about how your specific MintMe mining instance is wired together, especially given your command:

./webchain-miner -o pool.webchain.network:3333 -u 0xa733c9545a3476de410e7704d8be586d59db9c6d -p x

1. The Core Architecture

The miner is built on top of a few core systems:

The Foundation (src/App.cpp, src/xmrig.cpp, src/core/Controller.cpp): These files handle the entry point (main), parsing your command-line arguments (-o, -u, -p), and orchestrating the miner's lifecycle.

The Network Layer (src/net/, src/common/net/): This is where it connects to pool.webchain.network:3333. It uses the Stratum protocol (standard for mining pools) managed by files like Client.cpp, Pool.cpp, and Job.cpp. It relies on libuv for all asynchronous networking.

The "Workers" (src/workers/): Files like CpuThread.cpp, Worker.cpp, and Workers.cpp are responsible for spawning individual threads mapped to your CPU cores to do the actual mathematical lifting.

2. The Crypto: Why MintMe?

If you look closely at the SOURCES_CRYPTO section, you'll see this:

CMake
set(SOURCES_CRYPTO
    src/crypto/Lyra2.c
    src/crypto/Sponge.c
   )


This is the defining feature of this fork! Standard Monero/XMRig uses variations of CryptoNight or RandomX. Webchain (now MintMe) famously switched to a custom variant of Lyra2 (specifically Lyra2-webchain) to be ASIC-resistant and CPU-friendly.

When your CPU workers get a Job from the pool, they pass the data through the hashing functions defined in Lyra2.c and Sponge.c.

3. What Was Excluded/Included in Your Build

Because you built this on Ubuntu without changing any CMake options:

OS Support: It compiled src/App_unix.cpp, src/common/Platform_unix.cpp, src/Cpu_unix.cpp, and src/Mem_unix.cpp.

HTTP API: The option WITH_HTTPD is ON by default. That's why we had to spend all that time fixing the src/common/api/ files. The miner has a built-in REST API (powered by libmicrohttpd) to check hashrates, but because you didn't pass an API port in your command, it's currently dormant.

Other Algos: Options like WITH_AEON, WITH_SUMO, and WITH_IPBC default to OFF. The codebase still knows about CryptoNight, but the compiler stripped that code out to keep the binary lean for Lyra2.

CPU ID: WITH_LIBCPUID is ON. The miner compiled the 3rd party libcpuid library to accurately detect your processor's caches and features (like AES-NI or AVX2) to optimize the thread count and memory allocation automatically.

Where to go next?

Since you want to dissect how the miner actually mines MintMe based on your command, we should follow the flow of data.

I suggest we look at one of two places next:

src/xmrig.cpp or src/App.cpp: To see how your command-line arguments (-o, -u, -p) are parsed and how the main controller is initialized.

src/crypto/Lyra2.c (or src/common/crypto/Algorithm.cpp): If you want to dive straight into the mathematical heart of the MintMe hashing algorithm.

Which direction would you like to take?

#include <stdlib.h>

#include <uv.h>







#include "api/Api.h"

#include "App.h"

#include "common/Console.h"

#include "common/log/Log.h"

#include "common/Platform.h"

#include "core/Config.h"

#include "core/Controller.h"

#include "Cpu.h"

#include "Mem.h"

#include "net/Network.h"

#include "Summary.h"

#include "version.h"

#include "workers/Workers.h"







#ifndef XMRIG_NO_HTTPD

# include "common/api/Httpd.h"

#endif







App *App::m_self = nullptr;










App::App(int argc, char **argv) :

m_console(nullptr),

m_httpd(nullptr)

{

m_self = this;




m_controller = new xmrig::Controller();

if (m_controller->init(argc, argv) != 0) {

return;

}




if (!m_controller->config()->isBackground()) {

m_console = new Console(this);

}




uv_signal_init(uv_default_loop(), &m_sigHUP);

uv_signal_init(uv_default_loop(), &m_sigINT);

uv_signal_init(uv_default_loop(), &m_sigTERM);

}







App::~App()

{

uv_tty_reset_mode();




delete m_console;

delete m_controller;




# ifndef XMRIG_NO_HTTPD

delete m_httpd;

# endif

}







int App::exec()

{

if (!m_controller->isReady()) {

return 2;

}




uv_signal_start(&m_sigHUP, App::onSignal, SIGHUP);

uv_signal_start(&m_sigINT, App::onSignal, SIGINT);

uv_signal_start(&m_sigTERM, App::onSignal, SIGTERM);




background();




Mem::init(m_controller->config()->isHugePages());




Summary::print(m_controller);




if (m_controller->config()->isDryRun()) {

LOG_NOTICE("OK");

release();




return 0;

}




# ifndef XMRIG_NO_API

Api::start(m_controller);

# endif




# ifndef XMRIG_NO_HTTPD

m_httpd = new Httpd(

m_controller->config()->apiPort(),

m_controller->config()->apiToken(),

m_controller->config()->isApiIPv6(),

m_controller->config()->isApiRestricted()

);




m_httpd->start();

# endif




Workers::start(m_controller);




m_controller->network()->connect();




const int r = uv_run(uv_default_loop(), UV_RUN_DEFAULT);

uv_loop_close(uv_default_loop());




release();

return r;

}







void App::onConsoleCommand(char command)

{

switch (command) {

case 'h':

case 'H':

Workers::printHashrate(true);

break;




case 'p':

case 'P':

if (Workers::isEnabled()) {

LOG_INFO(m_controller->config()->isColors() ? "\x1B[01;33mpaused\x1B[0m, press \x1B[01;35mr\x1B[0m to resume" : "paused, press 'r' to resume");

Workers::setEnabled(false);

}

break;




case 'r':

case 'R':

if (!Workers::isEnabled()) {

LOG_INFO(m_controller->config()->isColors() ? "\x1B[01;32mresumed" : "resumed");

Workers::setEnabled(true);

}

break;




case 3:

LOG_WARN("Ctrl+C received, exiting");

close();

break;




default:

break;

}

}







void App::close()

{

m_controller->network()->stop();

Workers::stop();




uv_stop(uv_default_loop());

}







void App::release()

{

}







void App::onSignal(uv_signal_t *handle, int signum)

{

switch (signum)

{

case SIGHUP:

LOG_WARN("SIGHUP received, exiting");

break;




case SIGTERM:

LOG_WARN("SIGTERM received, exiting");

break;




case SIGINT:

LOG_WARN("SIGINT received, exiting");

break;




default:

break;

}




uv_signal_stop(handle);

m_self->close();

}

#include <stdlib.h>

#include <signal.h>

#include <errno.h>

#include <unistd.h>







#include "App.h"

#include "common/log/Log.h"

#include "core/Config.h"

#include "core/Controller.h"







void App::background()

{

signal(SIGPIPE, SIG_IGN);




if (!m_controller->config()->isBackground()) {

return;

}




int i = fork();

if (i < 0) {

exit(1);

}




if (i > 0) {

exit(0);

}




i = setsid();




if (i < 0) {

LOG_ERR("setsid() failed (errno = %d)", errno);

}




i = chdir("/");

if (i < 0) {

LOG_ERR("chdir() failed (errno = %d)", errno);

}

}

**Gemini:**
These files represent the "steering wheel" and "engine block" of the miner. Let’s break down exactly what happens chronologically when you hit Enter on your command:

./webchain-miner -o pool.webchain.network:3333 -u 0xa73... -p x

Step 1: The Entry Point (src/xmrig.cpp)

It doesn't get simpler than this. The OS calls main(), which immediately creates an instance of the App class, passing in your command-line arguments (argc and argv). It then calls app.exec() and waits for the program to finish.

Step 2: The Brain gets Initialized (src/App.cpp - Constructor)

Inside the App constructor, the very first thing it does is create the Controller:

C++
m_controller = new xmrig::Controller();
m_controller->init(argc, argv);


The Controller acts as the global state manager. When init() is called, it parses your -o, -u, and -p flags and stores them in a Config object.

Next, it checks if you want to run in the background (daemon mode). Since you didn't pass the -B flag, it creates a Console object, which allows you to type commands while the miner runs. Finally, it registers OS signals so the miner knows how to shut down gracefully if you close the terminal.

Step 3: The Pre-Flight Check (src/App.cpp - exec())

When app.exec() starts, the setup phase begins:

background(): Calls out to src/App_unix.cpp. It ignores SIGPIPE (preventing the app from crashing if a network socket unexpectedly closes) and would fork the process into a daemon if you had asked it to.

Mem::init(...): This is a crucial step for CPU mining. It attempts to allocate "HugePages" in your OS memory. Standard memory pages are 4KB, but HugePages are 2MB or larger. Using HugePages drastically reduces the overhead of memory lookups, which gives a massive boost to hashing speeds.

Summary::print(...): This prints the welcome text you see in the console (CPU info, memory info, pool address).

API Initialization: Because we compiled with WITH_HTTPD, it creates the Httpd object. But since you didn't provide an API port in your command or config.json, it likely just sits idle.

Step 4: Ignition!

These two lines are where the actual mining starts:

C++
Workers::start(m_controller);
m_controller->network()->connect();


Workers::start: Spawns multiple independent CPU threads (usually one per CPU core, or based on cache size). These threads sit in memory waiting for a job.

network()->connect(): Reaches out to pool.webchain.network:3333 using the Stratum protocol to say, "I am wallet 0xa73..., give me a job."

Step 5: The Infinite Event Loop

Finally, the miner executes this line:

C++
const int r = uv_run(uv_default_loop(), UV_RUN_DEFAULT);


This is the heartbeat of the application. The miner relies heavily on libuv, a highly efficient C library originally built for Node.js to handle asynchronous events.

Instead of writing complex, messy code to constantly check if the pool sent a new job or if you typed a command, uv_run takes over the main thread. It sits in a loop, sleeping efficiently until an event wakes it up:

Did the pool send a new block header? Wake up the network module!

Did you press 'h' on the keyboard? Trigger App::onConsoleCommand() to print the current hashrate.

Did you press 'p'? Pause the workers.

It stays stuck on this line until you press Ctrl+C. Once you do, App::onConsoleCommand() catches the signal, calls close(), stops the network, stops the workers, and gracefully breaks the uv_run loop.

Where to dissect next?

We have three main paths to follow from here based on how deep you want to go:

The Network (src/net/Network.h/cpp): How does it talk to the pool and get jobs?

The Workers (src/workers/Workers.h/cpp): How does it map threads to your CPU cores?

The Controller/Config (src/core/Controller.cpp): How exactly did it parse your command line arguments into usable data?

What is your preference?

#ifdef _MSC_VER

#pragma warning(disable:4244)

#endif




#include <inttypes.h>

#include <memory>

#include <time.h>







#include "api/Api.h"

#include "common/log/Log.h"

#include "common/net/Client.h"

#include "common/net/strategies/FailoverStrategy.h"

#include "common/net/strategies/SinglePoolStrategy.h"

#include "common/net/SubmitResult.h"

#include "core/Config.h"

#include "core/Controller.h"

#include "net/Network.h"

#include "net/strategies/DonateStrategy.h"

#include "workers/Workers.h"







Network::Network(xmrig::Controller *controller) :

m_donate(nullptr),

m_controller(controller)

{

srand(time(0) ^ (uintptr_t) this);




Workers::setListener(this);




const std::vector<Pool> &pools = controller->config()->pools();




if (pools.size() > 1) {

m_strategy = new FailoverStrategy(pools, controller->config()->retryPause(), controller->config()->retries(), this);

}

else {

m_strategy = new SinglePoolStrategy(pools.front(), controller->config()->retryPause(), controller->config()->retries(), this);

}




if (controller->config()->donateLevel() > 0) {

m_donate = new DonateStrategy(controller->config()->donateLevel(), controller->config()->pools().front().user(), controller->config()->algorithm().algo(), this);

}




m_timer.data = this;

uv_timer_init(uv_default_loop(), &m_timer);




uv_timer_start(&m_timer, Network::onTick, kTickInterval, kTickInterval);

}







Network::~Network()

{

}







void Network::connect()

{

m_strategy->connect();

}







void Network::stop()

{

if (m_donate) {

m_donate->stop();

}




m_strategy->stop();

}







void Network::onActive(IStrategy *strategy, Client *client)

{

if (m_donate && m_donate == strategy) {

LOG_NOTICE("dev donate started");

return;

}




m_state.setPool(client->host(), client->port(), client->ip());




LOG_INFO(isColors() ? "\x1B[01;37muse pool \x1B[01;36m%s:%d \x1B[01;30m%s" : "use pool %s:%d %s", client->host(), client->port(), client->ip());

}







void Network::onJob(IStrategy *strategy, Client *client, const Job &job)

{

if (m_donate && m_donate->isActive() && m_donate != strategy) {

return;

}




setJob(client, job, m_donate == strategy);

}







void Network::onJobResult(const JobResult &result)

{

if (result.poolId == -1 && m_donate) {

m_donate->submit(result);

return;

}




m_strategy->submit(result);

}







void Network::onPause(IStrategy *strategy)

{

if (m_donate && m_donate == strategy) {

LOG_NOTICE("dev donate finished");

m_strategy->resume();

}




if (!m_strategy->isActive()) {

LOG_ERR("no active pools, stop mining");

m_state.stop();

return Workers::pause();

}

}







void Network::onResultAccepted(IStrategy *strategy, Client *client, const SubmitResult &result, const char *error)

{

m_state.add(result, error);




if (error) {

LOG_INFO(isColors() ? "\x1B[01;31mrejected\x1B[0m (%" PRId64 "/%" PRId64 ") diff \x1B[01;37m%u\x1B[0m \x1B[31m\"%s\"\x1B[0m \x1B[01;30m(%" PRIu64 " ms)"

: "rejected (%" PRId64 "/%" PRId64 ") diff %u \"%s\" (%" PRIu64 " ms)",

m_state.accepted, m_state.rejected, result.diff, error, result.elapsed);

}

else {

LOG_INFO(isColors() ? "\x1B[01;32maccepted\x1B[0m (%" PRId64 "/%" PRId64 ") diff \x1B[01;37m%u\x1B[0m \x1B[01;30m(%" PRIu64 " ms)"

: "accepted (%" PRId64 "/%" PRId64 ") diff %u (%" PRIu64 " ms)",

m_state.accepted, m_state.rejected, result.diff, result.elapsed);

}

}







bool Network::isColors() const

{

return m_controller->config()->isColors();

}







void Network::setJob(Client *client, const Job &job, bool donate)

{

LOG_INFO(isColors() ? MAGENTA_BOLD("new job") " from " WHITE_BOLD("%s:%d") " diff " WHITE_BOLD("%d") " algo " WHITE_BOLD("%s")

: "new job from %s:%d diff %d algo %s",

client->host(), client->port(), job.diff(), job.algorithm().shortName());




m_state.diff = job.diff();

Workers::setJob(job, donate);

}







void Network::tick()

{

const uint64_t now = uv_now(uv_default_loop());




m_strategy->tick(now);




if (m_donate) {

m_donate->tick(now);

}




# ifndef XMRIG_NO_API

Api::tick(m_state);

# endif

}







void Network::onTick(uv_timer_t *handle)

{

static_cast<Network*>(handle->data)->tick();

}

src/net/Network.h:

#ifndef __NETWORK_H__

#define __NETWORK_H__







#include <vector>

#ifndef __JOBRESULT_H__

#define __JOBRESULT_H__







#include <memory.h>

#include <stdint.h>







#include "common/net/Job.h"







class JobResult

{

public:

inline JobResult() : poolId(0), diff(0), nonce(0) {}

inline JobResult(int poolId, const xmrig::Id &jobId, uint64_t nonce, const uint8_t *result, uint32_t diff, const xmrig::Algorithm &algorithm) :

poolId(poolId),

diff(diff),

nonce(nonce),

algorithm(algorithm),

jobId(jobId)

{

memcpy(this->result, result, sizeof(this->result));

}







inline uint64_t actualDiff() const

{

return Job::toDiff(reinterpret_cast<const uint64_t*>(result)[3]);

}







int poolId;

uint32_t diff;

uint64_t nonce;

uint8_t result[32];

xmrig::Algorithm algorithm;

xmrig::Id jobId;

};




#endif /* __JOBRESULT_H__ */

```

---

**Gemini:**
These files give us a beautiful look at the "traffic cop" of the miner. Network.cpp doesn't actually speak directly to the internet, nor does it calculate any hashes. Instead, it acts as the master coordinator between your Pool Connections (the outside world) and your CPU Workers (the mathematical engine).

Because your command only had one -o flag (-o pool.webchain.network:3333), here is exactly how Network.cpp routes your data.

1. The Setup: Choosing a Strategy

When App.cpp created the network layer, it called the Network::Network(...) constructor.

C++
    if (pools.size() > 1) {
        m_strategy = new FailoverStrategy(...);
    } else {
        m_strategy = new SinglePoolStrategy(...);
    }


Because you only provided one pool, it creates a SinglePoolStrategy. If you had provided multiple pools in a config.json, it would use FailoverStrategy to automatically switch to a backup pool if webchain.network goes offline.

It also checks your donation level. If the developer fee is active (which it usually is by default, around 1-5%), it spins up a completely separate background strategy (m_donate) that will occasionally steal the connection to mine for the developers.

Finally, it starts a 1-second stopwatch (m_timer) managed by libuv to run Network::tick() every second, making sure connections haven't timed out.

2. Inbound Traffic: The Job Pipeline

When App.cpp called network()->connect(), your SinglePoolStrategy reached out to the pool. Once the pool replies, the flow looks like this:

onActive(): The pool accepts your connection. Network logs that familiar white-and-cyan text: "use pool pool.webchain.network:3333".

onJob(): The pool sends you a "Job" (a block header that needs hashing).

setJob(): Network.cpp logs the magenta "new job" message, and then yells down to the engine room: Workers::setJob(job, ...).

At this point, Network.cpp's job is done for the moment. The CPU threads take that job and start furiously calculating hashes.

3. Outbound Traffic: The Result Pipeline

Eventually, one of your CPU cores finds a hash that meets the pool's difficulty requirements. When that happens, the worker packages the data into a JobResult (the header file you provided).

Think of JobResult.h as a winning lottery ticket. It contains:

poolId: Which pool this was for (in case we are mining for the devs right now).

jobId: So the pool knows which block you just solved.

nonce: The magic random number your CPU guessed to get the right answer.

result[32]: The actual 256-bit hash.

The worker hands this ticket back to the traffic cop via Network::onJobResult(const JobResult &result):

C++
    if (result.poolId == -1 && m_donate) {
        m_donate->submit(result); // This hash goes to the devs!
        return;
    }

    m_strategy->submit(result); // This hash goes to your wallet!


The strategy formats this into a JSON-RPC Stratum message and fires it over the internet to the pool.

4. The Receipt

When the pool receives your JobResult, it verifies the math. It then replies with a "Yes" or "No".

This triggers Network::onResultAccepted(). If the pool says "Yes", you get the green "accepted" message, and your pending balance goes up on the MintMe pool website. If your hash was stale (someone else solved the block first), you get the red "rejected" message.

Where does the rabbit hole go next?

We are sitting right in the middle of the pipeline. We can go:

"Up" towards the Internet: Look at src/common/net/Client.cpp or Pool.cpp to see how the Stratum protocol actually packs these messages into raw JSON strings over TCP sockets.

"Down" towards the Metal: Look at src/workers/Workers.cpp and Worker.cpp to see how it takes that Job and splits it across your physical CPU cores.

Which direction would you like to explore?

#include <assert.h>

#include "rapidjson/stringbuffer.h"

#include "rapidjson/writer.h"







#ifdef _MSC_VER

# define strncasecmp(x,y,z) _strnicmp(x,y,z)

#endif







int64_t Client::m_sequence = 1;

xmrig::Storage<Client> Client::m_storage;







Client::Client(int id, const char *agent, IClientListener *listener) :

m_ipv6(false),

m_nicehash(false),

m_quiet(false),

m_agent(agent),

m_listener(listener),

m_extensions(0),

m_id(id),

m_retries(5),

m_retryPause(5000),

m_failures(0),

m_recvBufPos(0),

m_state(UnconnectedState),

m_expire(0),

m_jobs(0),

m_keepAlive(0),

m_key(0),

m_stream(nullptr),

m_socket(nullptr)

{

m_key = m_storage.add(this);




memset(m_ip, 0, sizeof(m_ip));

memset(&m_hints, 0, sizeof(m_hints));




m_resolver.data = m_storage.ptr(m_key);




m_hints.ai_family = AF_UNSPEC;

m_hints.ai_socktype = SOCK_STREAM;

m_hints.ai_protocol = IPPROTO_TCP;




m_recvBuf.base = m_buf;

m_recvBuf.len = sizeof(m_buf);

}







Client::~Client()

{

delete m_socket;

}







void Client::connect()

{

resolve(m_pool.host());

}







/**

* @brief Connect to server.

*

* @param url

*/

void Client::connect(const Pool &url)

{

setPool(url);

connect();

}







void Client::deleteLater()

{

if (!m_listener) {

return;

}




m_listener = nullptr;




if (!disconnect()) {

m_storage.remove(m_key);

}

}







void Client::setPool(const Pool &pool)

{

if (!pool.isValid()) {

return;

}




m_pool = pool;

}







void Client::tick(uint64_t now)

{

if (m_state == ConnectedState) {

if (m_expire && now > m_expire) {

LOG_DEBUG_ERR("[%s] timeout", m_pool.url());

close();

}

else if (m_keepAlive && now > m_keepAlive) {

ping();

}

}




if (m_expire && now > m_expire && m_state == ConnectingState) {

connect();

}

}







bool Client::disconnect()

{

m_keepAlive = 0;

m_expire = 0;

m_failures = -1;




return close();

}







int64_t Client::submit(const JobResult &result)

{

using namespace rapidjson;




# ifdef XMRIG_PROXY_PROJECT

const char *nonce = result.nonce;

const char *data = result.result;

# else

char *nonce = m_sendBuf;

char *data = m_sendBuf + 18;




Job::toHex(reinterpret_cast<const unsigned char*>(&result.nonce), 8, nonce);

nonce[16] = '\0';




Job::toHex(result.result, 32, data);

data[64] = '\0';

# endif




Document doc(kObjectType);

auto &allocator = doc.GetAllocator();




doc.AddMember("id", m_sequence, allocator);

doc.AddMember("jsonrpc", "2.0", allocator);

doc.AddMember("method", "submit", allocator);

doc.AddMember("worker", StringRef(m_pool.workerId()), allocator);




Value params(kObjectType);

params.AddMember("id", StringRef(m_rpcId.data()), allocator);

params.AddMember("job_id", StringRef(result.jobId.data()), allocator);

params.AddMember("nonce", StringRef(nonce), allocator);

params.AddMember("result", StringRef(data), allocator);




if (m_extensions & AlgoExt) {

params.AddMember("algo", StringRef(result.algorithm.shortName()), allocator);

}




doc.AddMember("params", params, allocator);




# ifdef XMRIG_PROXY_PROJECT

m_results[m_sequence] = SubmitResult(m_sequence, result.diff, result.actualDiff(), result.id);

# else

m_results[m_sequence] = SubmitResult(m_sequence, result.diff, result.actualDiff());

# endif




return send(doc);

}







bool Client::close()

{

if (m_state == UnconnectedState || m_state == ClosingState || !m_socket) {

return false;

}




setState(ClosingState);




if (uv_is_closing(reinterpret_cast<uv_handle_t*>(m_socket)) == 0) {

uv_close(reinterpret_cast<uv_handle_t*>(m_socket), Client::onClose);

}




return true;

}







bool Client::isCriticalError(const char *message)

{

if (!message) {

return false;

}




if (strncasecmp(message, "Unauthenticated", 15) == 0) {

return true;

}




if (strncasecmp(message, "your IP is banned", 17) == 0) {

return true;

}




if (strncasecmp(message, "IP Address currently banned", 27) == 0) {

return true;

}




return false;

}







bool Client::parseJob(const rapidjson::Value &params, int *code)

{

if (!params.IsObject()) {

*code = 2;

return false;

}




Job job(m_id, m_nicehash, m_pool.algorithm(), m_rpcId);




if (!job.setId(params["job_id"].GetString())) {

*code = 3;

return false;

}




if (!job.setBlob(params["blob"].GetString())) {

*code = 4;

return false;

}




if (!job.setTarget(params["target"].GetString())) {

*code = 5;

return false;

}




if (params.HasMember("algo")) {

job.algorithm().parseAlgorithm(params["algo"].GetString());

}




if (params.HasMember("variant")) {

const rapidjson::Value &variant = params["variant"];




if (variant.IsInt()) {

job.algorithm().parseVariant(variant.GetInt());

}

else if (variant.IsString()){

job.algorithm().parseVariant(variant.GetString());

}

}




if (!verifyAlgorithm(job.algorithm())) {

*code = 6;




close();

return false;

}




if (m_job != job) {

m_jobs++;

m_job = std::move(job);

return true;

}




if (m_jobs == 0) { // https://github.com/xmrig/xmrig/issues/459

return false;

}




if (!isQuiet()) {

LOG_WARN("[%s] duplicate job received, reconnect", m_pool.url());

}




m_job = Job();




close();

return false;

}







bool Client::parseLogin(const rapidjson::Value &result, int *code)

{

if (!m_rpcId.setId(result["id"].GetString())) {

*code = 1;

return false;

}




m_nicehash = m_pool.isNicehash();




if (result.HasMember("extensions")) {

parseExtensions(result["extensions"]);

}




const bool rc = parseJob(result["job"], code);

m_jobs = 0;




return rc;

}







bool Client::verifyAlgorithm(const xmrig::Algorithm &algorithm) const

{

if (m_pool.isCompatible(algorithm)) {

return true;

}




if (isQuiet()) {

return false;

}




if (algorithm.isValid()) {

LOG_ERR("Incompatible algorithm \"%s\" detected, reconnect", algorithm.name());

}

else {

LOG_ERR("Unknown/unsupported algorithm detected, reconnect");

}




return false;

}







int Client::resolve(const char *host)

{

setState(HostLookupState);




m_expire = 0;

m_recvBufPos = 0;




if (m_failures == -1) {

m_failures = 0;

}




const int r = uv_getaddrinfo(uv_default_loop(), &m_resolver, Client::onResolved, host, nullptr, &m_hints);

if (r) {

if (!isQuiet()) {

LOG_ERR("[%s:%u] getaddrinfo error: \"%s\"", host, m_pool.port(), uv_strerror(r));

}

return 1;

}




return 0;

}







int64_t Client::send(const rapidjson::Document &doc)

{

using namespace rapidjson;




StringBuffer buffer(0, 512);

Writer<StringBuffer> writer(buffer);

doc.Accept(writer);




const size_t size = buffer.GetSize();

if (size > (sizeof(m_buf) - 2)) {

return -1;

}




memcpy(m_sendBuf, buffer.GetString(), size);

m_sendBuf[size] = '\n';

m_sendBuf[size + 1] = '\0';




return send(size + 1);

}







int64_t Client::send(size_t size)

{

LOG_DEBUG("[%s] send (%d bytes): \"%s\"", m_pool.url(), size, m_sendBuf);

if (state() != ConnectedState || !uv_is_writable(m_stream)) {

LOG_DEBUG_ERR("[%s] send failed, invalid state: %d", m_pool.url(), m_state);

return -1;

}




uv_buf_t buf = uv_buf_init(m_sendBuf, (unsigned int) size);




if (uv_try_write(m_stream, &buf, 1) < 0) {

close();

return -1;

}




m_expire = uv_now(uv_default_loop()) + kResponseTimeout;

return m_sequence++;

}







void Client::connect(const std::vector<addrinfo*> &ipv4, const std::vector<addrinfo*> &ipv6)

{

addrinfo *addr = nullptr;

m_ipv6 = ipv4.empty() && !ipv6.empty();




if (m_ipv6) {

addr = ipv6[ipv6.size() == 1 ? 0 : rand() % ipv6.size()];

uv_ip6_name(reinterpret_cast<sockaddr_in6*>(addr->ai_addr), m_ip, 45);

}

else {

addr = ipv4[ipv4.size() == 1 ? 0 : rand() % ipv4.size()];

uv_ip4_name(reinterpret_cast<sockaddr_in*>(addr->ai_addr), m_ip, 16);

}




connect(addr->ai_addr);

}







void Client::connect(sockaddr *addr)

{

setState(ConnectingState);




reinterpret_cast<sockaddr_in*>(addr)->sin_port = htons(m_pool.port());

delete m_socket;




uv_connect_t *req = new uv_connect_t;

req->data = m_storage.ptr(m_key);




m_socket = new uv_tcp_t;

m_socket->data = m_storage.ptr(m_key);




uv_tcp_init(uv_default_loop(), m_socket);

uv_tcp_nodelay(m_socket, 1);




# ifndef WIN32

uv_tcp_keepalive(m_socket, 1, 60);

# endif




uv_tcp_connect(req, m_socket, reinterpret_cast<const sockaddr*>(addr), Client::onConnect);

}







void Client::login()

{

using namespace rapidjson;

m_results.clear();




Document doc(kObjectType);

auto &allocator = doc.GetAllocator();




doc.AddMember("id", 1, allocator);

doc.AddMember("jsonrpc", "2.0", allocator);

doc.AddMember("method", "login", allocator);

doc.AddMember("worker", StringRef(m_pool.workerId()), allocator);




Value params(kObjectType);

params.AddMember("login", StringRef(m_pool.user()), allocator);

params.AddMember("pass", StringRef(m_pool.password()), allocator);

params.AddMember("agent", StringRef(m_agent), allocator);




if (m_pool.rigId()) {

params.AddMember("rigid", StringRef(m_pool.rigId()), allocator);

}




Value algo(kArrayType);




for (const auto &a : m_pool.algorithms()) {

algo.PushBack(StringRef(a.shortName()), allocator);

}




//params.AddMember("algo", algo, allocator);

doc.AddMember("params", params, allocator);




send(doc);

}







void Client::onClose()

{

delete m_socket;




m_stream = nullptr;

m_socket = nullptr;

setState(UnconnectedState);




reconnect();

}







void Client::parse(char *line, size_t len)

{

startTimeout();




line[len - 1] = '\0';




LOG_DEBUG("[%s] received (%d bytes): \"%s\"", m_pool.url(), len, line);




if (len < 32 || line[0] != '{') {

if (!isQuiet()) {

LOG_ERR("[%s] JSON decode failed", m_pool.url());

}




return;

}




rapidjson::Document doc;

if (doc.ParseInsitu(line).HasParseError()) {

if (!isQuiet()) {

LOG_ERR("[%s] JSON decode failed: \"%s\"", m_pool.url(), rapidjson::GetParseError_En(doc.GetParseError()));

}




return;

}




if (!doc.IsObject()) {

return;

}




const rapidjson::Value &id = doc["id"];

if (id.IsInt64()) {

parseResponse(id.GetInt64(), doc["result"], doc["error"]);

}

else {

parseNotification(doc["method"].GetString(), doc["params"], doc["error"]);

}

}







void Client::parseExtensions(const rapidjson::Value &value)

{

m_extensions = 0;




if (!value.IsArray()) {

return;

}




for (const rapidjson::Value &ext : value.GetArray()) {

if (!ext.IsString()) {

continue;

}




if (strcmp(ext.GetString(), "algo") == 0) {

m_extensions |= AlgoExt;

continue;

}




if (strcmp(ext.GetString(), "nicehash") == 0) {

m_extensions |= NicehashExt;

m_nicehash = true;

continue;

}

}

}







void Client::parseNotification(const char *method, const rapidjson::Value &params, const rapidjson::Value &error)

{

if (error.IsObject()) {

if (!isQuiet()) {

LOG_ERR("[%s] error: \"%s\", code: %d", m_pool.url(), error["message"].GetString(), error["code"].GetInt());

}

return;

}




if (!method) {

return;

}




if (strcmp(method, "job") == 0) {

int code = -1;

if (parseJob(params, &code)) {

m_listener->onJobReceived(this, m_job);

}




return;

}




LOG_WARN("[%s] unsupported method: \"%s\"", m_pool.url(), method);

}







void Client::parseResponse(int64_t id, const rapidjson::Value &result, const rapidjson::Value &error)

{

if (error.IsObject()) {

const char *message = error["message"].GetString();




auto it = m_results.find(id);

if (it != m_results.end()) {

it->second.done();

m_listener->onResultAccepted(this, it->second, message);

m_results.erase(it);

}

else if (!isQuiet()) {

LOG_ERR("[%s] error: \"%s\", code: %d", m_pool.url(), message, error["code"].GetInt());

}




if (isCriticalError(message)) {

close();

}




return;

}




if (!result.IsObject()) {

return;

}




if (id == 1) {

int code = -1;

if (!parseLogin(result, &code)) {

if (!isQuiet()) {

LOG_ERR("[%s] login error code: %d", m_pool.url(), code);

}




close();

return;

}




m_failures = 0;

m_listener->onLoginSuccess(this);

m_listener->onJobReceived(this, m_job);

return;

}




auto it = m_results.find(id);

if (it != m_results.end()) {

it->second.done();

m_listener->onResultAccepted(this, it->second, nullptr);

m_results.erase(it);

}

}







void Client::ping()

{

send(snprintf(m_sendBuf, sizeof(m_sendBuf), "{\"id\":%" PRId64 ",\"jsonrpc\":\"2.0\",\"method\":\"keepalived\",\"params\":{\"id\":\"%s\"}}\n", m_sequence, m_rpcId.data()));

}







void Client::reconnect()

{

if (!m_listener) {

m_storage.remove(m_key);




return;

}




setState(ConnectingState);

m_keepAlive = 0;




if (m_failures == -1) {

return m_listener->onClose(this, -1);

}




m_failures++;

m_listener->onClose(this, (int) m_failures);




m_expire = uv_now(uv_default_loop()) + m_retryPause;

}







void Client::setState(SocketState state)

{

LOG_DEBUG("[%s] state: %d", m_pool.url(), state);




if (m_state == state) {

return;

}




m_state = state;

}







void Client::startTimeout()

{

m_expire = 0;




if (m_pool.keepAlive()) {

m_keepAlive = uv_now(uv_default_loop()) + (m_pool.keepAlive() * 1000);

}

}







void Client::onAllocBuffer(uv_handle_t *handle, size_t suggested_size, uv_buf_t *buf)

{

auto client = getClient(handle->data);

if (!client) {

return;

}




buf->base = &client->m_recvBuf.base[client->m_recvBufPos];

buf->len = client->m_recvBuf.len - client->m_recvBufPos;

}







void Client::onClose(uv_handle_t *handle)

{

auto client = getClient(handle->data);

if (!client) {

return;

}




client->onClose();

}







void Client::onConnect(uv_connect_t *req, int status)

{

auto client = getClient(req->data);

if (!client) {

delete req;

return;

}




if (status < 0) {

if (!client->isQuiet()) {

LOG_ERR("[%s] connect error: \"%s\"", client->m_pool.url(), uv_strerror(status));

}




delete req;

client->close();

return;

}




client->m_stream = static_cast<uv_stream_t*>(req->handle);

client->m_stream->data = req->data;

client->setState(ConnectedState);




uv_read_start(client->m_stream, Client::onAllocBuffer, Client::onRead);

delete req;




client->login();

}







void Client::onRead(uv_stream_t *stream, ssize_t nread, const uv_buf_t *buf)

{

auto client = getClient(stream->data);

if (!client) {

return;

}




if (nread < 0) {

if (nread != UV_EOF && !client->isQuiet()) {

LOG_ERR("[%s] read error: \"%s\"", client->m_pool.url(), uv_strerror((int) nread));

}




client->close();

return;

}




if ((size_t) nread > (sizeof(m_buf) - 8 - client->m_recvBufPos)) {

client->close();

return;

}




assert(client->m_listener != nullptr);

if (!client->m_listener) {

return client->reconnect();

}




client->m_recvBufPos += nread;




char* end;

char* start = client->m_recvBuf.base;

size_t remaining = client->m_recvBufPos;




while ((end = static_cast<char*>(memchr(start, '\n', remaining))) != nullptr) {

end++;

size_t len = end - start;

client->parse(start, len);




remaining -= len;

start = end;

}




if (remaining == 0) {

client->m_recvBufPos = 0;

return;

}




if (start == client->m_recvBuf.base) {

return;

}




memcpy(client->m_recvBuf.base, start, remaining);

client->m_recvBufPos = remaining;

}







void Client::onResolved(uv_getaddrinfo_t *req, int status, struct addrinfo *res)

{

auto client = getClient(req->data);

if (!client) {

return;

}




assert(client->m_listener != nullptr);

if (!client->m_listener) {

return client->reconnect();

}




if (status < 0) {

if (!client->isQuiet()) {

LOG_ERR("[%s] DNS error: \"%s\"", client->m_pool.url(), uv_strerror(status));

}




return client->reconnect();

}




addrinfo *ptr = res;

std::vector<addrinfo*> ipv4;

std::vector<addrinfo*> ipv6;




while (ptr != nullptr) {

if (ptr->ai_family == AF_INET) {

ipv4.push_back(ptr);

}




if (ptr->ai_family == AF_INET6) {

ipv6.push_back(ptr);

}




ptr = ptr->ai_next;

}




if (ipv4.empty() && ipv6.empty()) {

if (!client->isQuiet()) {

LOG_ERR("[%s] DNS error: \"No IPv4 (A) or IPv6 (AAAA) records found\"", client->m_pool.url());

}




uv_freeaddrinfo(res);

return client->reconnect();

}




client->connect(ipv4, ipv6);

uv_freeaddrinfo(res);

}

#ifndef __CLIENT_H__

#define __CLIENT_H__







#include <map>

#include <uv.h>

---

**Gemini:**
This is the actual bridge to the outside world! Client.cpp is a fantastic example of a non-blocking network client. Because mining requires maximizing CPU time for hashing, this file is written to use almost zero CPU. It relies heavily on libuv to handle network events in the background and rapidjson to quickly assemble and tear down the JSON messages.

This entire file speaks Stratum, the standard JSON-RPC language used by almost all crypto mining pools.

Here is exactly what happens in this file when you run your command:
./webchain-miner -o pool.webchain.network:3333 -u 0xa73... -p x

1. The Handshake: DNS to TCP

When Network.cpp tells this client to connect, it doesn't just open a socket.

resolve("pool.webchain.network"): It asks your OS to look up the IP address for the pool asynchronously using uv_getaddrinfo.

onResolved(): Once the OS finds the IP, this callback fires. It handles both IPv4 and IPv6 routing.

connect(sockaddr): It creates a raw uv_tcp_t socket, sets it to port 3333, and initiates the connection.

onConnect(): When the TCP handshake finishes, it immediately triggers the login() function.

2. Logging In (Your Wallet Address)

Look at the Client::login() function. This is where your command-line arguments finally get packaged into a raw internet payload. It builds a JSON object that looks exactly like this:

JSON
{
  "id": 1,
  "jsonrpc": "2.0",
  "method": "login",
  "params": {
    "login": "0xa733c9545a3476de410e7704d8be586d59db9c6d",
    "pass": "x",
    "agent": "webchain-miner/2.8.0" 
  }
}


It converts that JSON to a string, slaps a \n (newline) at the end of it, and pushes it into the TCP socket using send().

3. Receiving the Work (The "Job")

Because it's a TCP stream, data arrives in chunks. The onRead() function grabs bytes from your network card as they arrive. It cleverly looks for the \n character. Once it finds a full line, it sends it to parse().

The pool responds to your login with a success message and immediately follows up with a "Job" notification.
The parseJob() function intercepts this. It pulls out:

job_id: The pool's internal tracker for this block.

blob: The actual block header data that needs to be hashed.

target: A hex string representing the difficulty. This is the mathematical threshold your hash must beat for the pool to accept it.

Once unpacked, it passes this Job object back up the chain to Network.cpp, which passes it to your CPU workers.

4. Submitting the Winning Ticket

When your CPU actually solves the math problem (which we haven't looked at yet), it hands a JobResult back to this client.

The Client::submit() function springs into action. It builds another JSON object:

JSON
{
  "id": 2,
  "jsonrpc": "2.0",
  "method": "submit",
  "params": {
    "id": "<your_rpc_id_from_login>",
    "job_id": "<the_job_id>",
    "nonce": "1a2b3c4d",
    "result": "<the_actual_winning_hash>"
  }
}


Notice how it uses m_sequence for the "id" field. It increments this number every time you send a hash. It then saves this sequence number in memory (m_results[m_sequence] = SubmitResult(...)).

When the pool replies a few milliseconds later with {"id": 2, "result": {"status": "OK"}}, the parseResponse() function matches the ID 2 to your saved ticket, clears it from memory, and fires the green "accepted" message!

We are at a crossroads!

We've traced the entire data pipeline from the internet, through the parser, and right up to the edge of the CPU thread. We have two places left to look to complete the picture:

Down to the Metal (src/workers/Workers.cpp & Worker.cpp): How does it pin threads to your specific CPU cores to maximize cache usage?

Into the Math (src/crypto/Lyra2.c & Algorithm.cpp): What exactly is your CPU doing to that blob of data to turn it into a winning hash?

Which sounds more interesting to you?

#include <assert.h>

#include <string.h>







#include "common/net/Job.h"







static inline unsigned char hf_hex2bin(char c, bool &err)

{

    if (c >= '0' && c <= '9') {

        return c - '0';

    }

    else if (c >= 'a' && c <= 'f') {

        return c - 'a' + 0xA;

    }

    else if (c >= 'A' && c <= 'F') {

        return c - 'A' + 0xA;

    }




    err = true;

    return 0;

}







static inline char hf_bin2hex(unsigned char c)

{

    if (c <= 0x9) {

        return '0' + c;

    }




    return 'a' - 0xA + c;

}







Job::Job() :

    m_nicehash(false),

    m_poolId(-2),

    m_threadId(-1),

    m_size(0),

    m_diff(0),

    m_target(0),

    m_blob()

{

}







Job::Job(int poolId, bool nicehash, xmrig::Algorithm algorithm, const xmrig::Id &clientId) :

    m_nicehash(nicehash),

    m_poolId(poolId),

    m_threadId(-1),

    m_size(0),

    m_diff(0),

    m_target(0),

    m_blob(),

    m_algorithm(algorithm),

    m_clientId(clientId)

{

}







Job::~Job()

{

}







bool Job::setBlob(const char *blob)

{

    if (!blob) {

        return false;

    }




    m_size = strlen(blob);

    if (m_size % 2 != 0) {

        return false;

    }




    m_size /= 2;

    if (m_size < 76 || m_size >= BLOB_SIZE) {

        return false;

    }




    if (!fromHex(blob, (int) m_size * 2, m_blob)) {

        return false;

    }




    if (*nonce() != 0 && !m_nicehash) {

        m_nicehash = true;

    }




#   ifdef XMRIG_PROXY_PROJECT

    memset(m_rawBlob, 0, sizeof(m_rawBlob));

    memcpy(m_rawBlob, blob, m_size * 2);

#   endif




    return true;

}







bool Job::setTarget(const char *target)

{

    if (!target) {

        return false;

    }




    const size_t len = strlen(target);




    if (len <= 8) {

        uint32_t tmp = 0;

        char str[8];

        memcpy(str, target, len);




        if (!fromHex(str, 8, reinterpret_cast<unsigned char*>(&tmp)) || tmp == 0) {

            return false;

        }




        m_target = 0xFFFFFFFFFFFFFFFFULL / (0xFFFFFFFFULL / static_cast<uint64_t>(tmp));

    }

    else if (len <= 16) {

        m_target = 0;

        char str[16];

        memcpy(str, target, len);




        if (!fromHex(str, 16, reinterpret_cast<unsigned char*>(&m_target)) || m_target == 0) {

            return false;

        }

    }

    else {

        return false;

    }




#   ifdef XMRIG_PROXY_PROJECT

    memset(m_rawTarget, 0, sizeof(m_rawTarget));

    memcpy(m_rawTarget, target, len);

#   endif




    m_diff = toDiff(m_target);

    return true;

}







xmrig::Variant Job::variant() const

{

    if (m_algorithm.algo() == xmrig::LYRA2 || m_algorithm.algo() == xmrig::LYRA2v2) {

        return xmrig::VARIANT_0;

    }




    return m_algorithm.variant();

}







bool Job::fromHex(const char* in, unsigned int len, unsigned char* out)

{

    bool error = false;

    for (unsigned int i = 0; i < len; i += 2) {

        out[i / 2] = (hf_hex2bin(in[i], error) << 4) | hf_hex2bin(in[i + 1], error);




        if (error) {

            return false;

        }

    }

    return true;

}







void Job::toHex(const unsigned char* in, unsigned int len, char* out)

{

    for (unsigned int i = 0; i < len; i++) {

        out[i * 2] = hf_bin2hex((in[i] & 0xF0) >> 4);

        out[i * 2 + 1] = hf_bin2hex(in[i] & 0x0F);

    }

}







#ifdef APP_DEBUG

char *Job::toHex(const unsigned char* in, unsigned int len)

{

    char *out = new char[len * 2 + 1]();

    toHex(in, len, out);




    return out;

}

#endif







bool Job::operator==(const Job &other) const

{

    return m_id == other.m_id && memcmp(m_blob, other.m_blob, m_size) == 0;

}







bool Job::operator!=(const Job &other) const

{

    return m_id != other.m_id || memcmp(m_blob, other.m_blob, m_size) != 0;

}

    inline bool isNicehash() const                    { return m_nicehash; }

    inline bool isValid() const                       { return m_size > 0 && m_diff > 0; }

    inline bool setId(const char *id)                 { return m_id.setId(id); }

    inline const uint64_t *nonce() const              { return reinterpret_cast<const uint64_t*>(m_blob + m_size - 8); }

    inline const uint8_t *blob() const                { return m_blob; }

    inline const xmrig::Algorithm &algorithm() const  { return m_algorithm; }

    inline const xmrig::Id &clientId() const          { return m_clientId; }

    inline int threadId() const                       { return m_threadId; }

    inline uint64_t *nonce()                          { return reinterpret_cast<uint64_t*>(m_blob + m_size - 8); }

    inline uint32_t diff() const                      { return static_cast<uint32_t>(m_diff); }

    inline uint64_t target() const                    { return m_target; }

    inline void setPoolId(int poolId)                 { m_poolId = poolId; }

#   endif

    static inline uint64_t *nonce(uint8_t *blob, size_t size) { return reinterpret_cast<uint64_t*>(blob + size - 8); }

    static inline uint64_t toDiff(uint64_t target) { return 0xFFFFFFFFFFFFFFFFULL / target; }

#   endif

private:

#include <assert.h>

#include <string.h>

#include <stdlib.h>

#include <stdio.h>







#include "common/net/Pool.h"

#include "rapidjson/document.h"







#ifdef APP_DEBUG

#   include "common/log/Log.h"

#endif







#ifdef _MSC_VER

#   define strncasecmp _strnicmp

#   define strcasecmp  _stricmp

#endif







Pool::Pool() :

    m_nicehash(false),

    m_keepAlive(0),

    m_port(kDefaultPort)

{

}







/**

 * @brief Parse url.

 *

 * Valid urls:

 * example.com

 * example.com:3333

 * stratum+tcp://example.com

 * stratum+tcp://example.com:3333

 *

 * @param url

 */

Pool::Pool(const char *url) :

    m_nicehash(false),

    m_keepAlive(0),

    m_port(kDefaultPort)

{

    parse(url);

}







Pool::Pool(const char *host, uint16_t port, const char *user, const char *password, int keepAlive, bool nicehash) :

    m_nicehash(nicehash),

    m_keepAlive(keepAlive),

    m_port(port),

    m_host(host),

    m_password(password),

    m_user(user)

{

    const size_t size = m_host.size() + 8;

    assert(size > 8);




    char *url = new char[size]();

    snprintf(url, size - 1, "%s:%d", m_host.data(), m_port);




    m_url = url;

}







bool Pool::isCompatible(const xmrig::Algorithm &algorithm) const

{

    return true; // TODO

    if (m_algorithms.empty()) {

        return true;

    }




    for (const auto &a : m_algorithms) {

        if (algorithm.algo() == a.algo()) {

            return true;

        }

    }




    return false;

}







bool Pool::isEqual(const Pool &other) const

{

    return (m_nicehash     == other.m_nicehash

            && m_keepAlive == other.m_keepAlive

            && m_port      == other.m_port

            && m_algorithm == other.m_algorithm

            && m_host      == other.m_host

            && m_password  == other.m_password

            && m_rigId     == other.m_rigId

            && m_url       == other.m_url

            && m_user      == other.m_user);

}







bool Pool::parse(const char *url)

{

    assert(url != nullptr);




    const char *p = strstr(url, "://");

    const char *base = url;




    if (p) {

        if (strncasecmp(url, "stratum+tcp://", 14)) {

            return false;

        }




        base = url + 14;

    }




    if (!strlen(base) || *base == '/') {

        return false;

    }




    m_url = url;

    if (base[0] == '[') {

        return parseIPv6(base);

    }




    const char *port = strchr(base, ':');

    if (!port) {

        m_host = base;

        return true;

    }




    const size_t size = port++ - base + 1;

    char *host        = new char[size]();

    memcpy(host, base, size - 1);




    m_host = host;

    m_port = static_cast<uint16_t>(strtol(port, nullptr, 10));




    return true;

}







bool Pool::setUserpass(const char *userpass)

{

    const char *p = strchr(userpass, ':');

    if (!p) {

        return false;

    }




    char *user = new char[p - userpass + 1]();

    strncpy(user, userpass, p - userpass);




    m_user     = user;

    m_password = p + 1;




    return true;

}







rapidjson::Value Pool::toJSON(rapidjson::Document &doc) const

{

    using namespace rapidjson;




    auto &allocator = doc.GetAllocator();




    Value obj(kObjectType);




    obj.AddMember("url",    StringRef(url()), allocator);

    obj.AddMember("user",   StringRef(user()), allocator);

    obj.AddMember("pass",   StringRef(password()), allocator);

    obj.AddMember("rig-id", rigId() ? Value(StringRef(rigId())).Move() : Value(kNullType).Move(), allocator);




#   ifndef XMRIG_PROXY_PROJECT

    obj.AddMember("nicehash", isNicehash(), allocator);

#   endif




    if (m_keepAlive == 0 || m_keepAlive == kKeepAliveTimeout) {

        obj.AddMember("keepalive", m_keepAlive > 0, allocator);

    }

    else {

        obj.AddMember("keepalive", m_keepAlive, allocator);

    }




    switch (m_algorithm.variant()) {

    case xmrig::VARIANT_AUTO:

    case xmrig::VARIANT_0:

    case xmrig::VARIANT_1:

        obj.AddMember("variant", m_algorithm.variant(), allocator);

        break;




    default:

        obj.AddMember("variant", StringRef(m_algorithm.variantName()), allocator);

        break;

    }




    return obj;

}







void Pool::adjust(xmrig::Algo algorithm)

{

    if (!isValid()) {

        return;

    }




    if (!m_algorithm.isValid()) {

        m_algorithm.setAlgo(algorithm);




        if (m_algorithm.variant() == xmrig::VARIANT_AUTO) {

            if (algorithm == xmrig::LYRA2 || algorithm == xmrig::LYRA2v2)  {

                m_algorithm.setVariant(xmrig::VARIANT_0);

            }

        }

    }




    if (strstr(m_host.data(), ".nicehash.com")) {

        m_keepAlive = false;

        m_nicehash  = true;




        if (strstr(m_host.data(), "cryptonightv7.")) {

            m_algorithm.setVariant(xmrig::VARIANT_1);

        }

    }




    if (strstr(m_host.data(), ".minergate.com")) {

        m_keepAlive = false;

        m_algorithm.setVariant(xmrig::VARIANT_1);

    }




    m_algorithms.push_back(m_algorithm);




#   ifndef XMRIG_PROXY_PROJECT

    if (m_algorithm.algo() != xmrig::LYRA2) {

        addVariant(xmrig::VARIANT_1);

        addVariant(xmrig::VARIANT_0);

    }

#   endif

}







#ifdef APP_DEBUG

void Pool::print() const

{

    LOG_NOTICE("url:       %s", m_url.data());

    LOG_DEBUG ("host:      %s", m_host.data());

    LOG_DEBUG ("port:      %d", static_cast<int>(m_port));

    LOG_DEBUG ("user:      %s", m_user.data());

    LOG_DEBUG ("pass:      %s", m_password.data());

    LOG_DEBUG ("rig-id     %s", m_rigId.data());

    LOG_DEBUG ("algo:      %s", m_algorithm.name());

    LOG_DEBUG ("nicehash:  %d", static_cast<int>(m_nicehash));

    LOG_DEBUG ("keepAlive: %d", m_keepAlive);

}

#endif







bool Pool::parseIPv6(const char *addr)

{

    const char *end = strchr(addr, ']');

    if (!end) {

        return false;

    }




    const char *port = strchr(end, ':');

    if (!port) {

        return false;

    }




    const size_t size = end - addr;

    char *host        = new char[size]();

    memcpy(host, addr + 1, size - 1);




    m_host = host;

    m_port = static_cast<uint16_t>(strtol(port + 1, nullptr, 10));




    return true;

}







void Pool::addVariant(xmrig::Variant variant)

{

    const xmrig::Algorithm algorithm(m_algorithm.algo(), variant);

    if (!algorithm.isValid() || m_algorithm == algorithm) {

        return;

    }




    m_algorithms.push_back(algorithm);

}

#ifndef __POOL_H__

#define __POOL_H__







#include <vector>







#include "common/crypto/Algorithm.h"

       );

    inline const xmrig::Algorithm &algorithm() const   { return m_algorithm; }

    inline const xmrig::Algorithms &algorithms() const { return m_algorithms; }

    inline uint16_t port() const                       { return m_port; }

    inline void setKeepAlive(int keepAlive)            { m_keepAlive = keepAlive >= 0 ? keepAlive : 0; }

    void adjust(xmrig::Algo algorithm);




#   ifdef APP_DEBUG

    void print() const;

#   endif




private:

    void addVariant(xmrig::Variant variant);

src/common/net/Storage.h:

```

#ifndef __STORAGE_H__

#define __STORAGE_H__







#include <assert.h>

#include <map>







namespace xmrig {







template <class TYPE>

class Storage

{

public:

    inline Storage() :

        m_counter(0)

    {

    }







    inline uintptr_t add(TYPE *ptr)

    {

        m_data[m_counter] = ptr;




        return m_counter++;

    }







    inline static void *ptr(uintptr_t id) { return reinterpret_cast<void *>(id); }







    inline TYPE *get(void *id) const { return get(reinterpret_cast<uintptr_t>(id)); }

    inline TYPE *get(uintptr_t id) const

    {

        assert(m_data.count(id) > 0);




        if (m_data.count(id) == 0) {

            return nullptr;

        }




        return m_data.at(id);

    }







    inline void remove(void *id) { remove(reinterpret_cast<uintptr_t>(id)); }

    inline void remove(uintptr_t id)

    {

        TYPE *obj = get(id);

        if (obj == nullptr) {

            return;

        }




        auto it = m_data.find(id);

        if (it != m_data.end()) {

            m_data.erase(it);

        }




        delete obj;

    }







private:

    std::map<uintptr_t, TYPE *> m_data;

    uint64_t m_counter;

};







} /* namespace xmrig */







#endif /* __STORAGE_H__ */

#include <uv.h>







#include "common/net/SubmitResult.h"







SubmitResult::SubmitResult(int64_t seq, uint32_t diff, uint64_t actualDiff, int64_t reqId) :

    reqId(reqId),

    seq(seq),

    diff(diff),

    actualDiff(actualDiff),

    elapsed(0)

{

    start = uv_hrtime();

}







void SubmitResult::done()

{

    elapsed = (uv_hrtime() - start) / 1000000;

}

src/common/net/Id.h:

```

#ifndef __ID_H__

#define __ID_H__







#include <string.h>







namespace xmrig {







class Id

{

public:

    inline Id() :

        m_data()

    {

    }







    inline Id(const char *id, size_t sizeFix = 0)

    {

        setId(id, sizeFix);

    }







    inline bool operator==(const Id &other) const

    {

        return memcmp(m_data, other.m_data, sizeof(m_data)) == 0;

    }







    inline bool operator!=(const Id &other) const

    {

        return memcmp(m_data, other.m_data, sizeof(m_data)) != 0;

    }







    Id &operator=(const Id &other)

    {

        memcpy(m_data, other.m_data, sizeof(m_data));




        return *this;

    }







    inline bool setId(const char *id, size_t sizeFix = 0)

    {

        memset(m_data, 0, sizeof(m_data));

        if (!id) {

            return false;

        }




        const size_t size = strlen(id);

        if (size >= sizeof(m_data)) {

            return false;

        }




        memcpy(m_data, id, size - sizeFix);

        return true;

    }







    inline const char *data() const { return m_data; }

    inline bool isValid() const     { return *m_data != '\0'; }







private:

    char m_data[64];

};







} /* namespace xmrig */







#endif /* __ID_H__ */

---

**Gemini:**
These files represent the Data Models—the actual cargo being transported by the network pipeline we just explored. If Network.cpp is the traffic cop and Client.cpp is the delivery truck, these files define the packages they are moving.

Here is the breakdown of what these data structures do under the hood.

1. Pool.cpp: The Address Book

When you passed -o pool.webchain.network:3333 into your command line, the Controller shoved that string into a Pool object.

The parse() method's job is to slice that string up. It looks for prefixes like stratum+tcp://, strips them away, separates the host from the port, and checks if it's an IPv6 address (wrapped in []). It also handles pool-specific quirks, like automatically adjusting settings if it detects you are connecting to nicehash.com or minergate.com.

2. Job.cpp: The Blueprint (The most important file here)

This is the holy grail for your CPU. When the pool sends a JSON "job" notification, Client.cpp instantiates a Job object. This object does heavy data translation so your CPU doesn't have to waste time doing it later.

setBlob(): The pool sends the block header (the "blob") as a hexadecimal string (e.g., "010b...a4"). Strings are slow to read and hash. This function loops through that string and converts every two characters into a raw, 1-byte binary integer, storing it in the m_blob array.

The nonce() pointer: Notice this clever inline function:
reinterpret_cast<uint64_t*>(m_blob + m_size - 8)

In Proof of Work mining, the "nonce" (number used once) is the arbitrary number your CPU guesses over and over to change the hash result. This code creates a pointer aimed exactly at the last 8 bytes of the blob. When your CPU thread runs, it will increment the value at this exact memory address before calculating the next hash.

setTarget(): The pool sends a "target" (difficulty). Job.cpp converts this into a 64-bit integer limit. The formula it uses calculates the maximum allowable hash value to be accepted by the pool:

Target=
Difficulty
2
32
−1
	​

2
64
−1
	​


Any hash your CPU generates that is numerically less than m_target is a valid share!

3. SubmitResult.cpp: The Stopwatch

Remember the console log we looked at earlier?
accepted (1/0) diff 100 (45 ms)

SubmitResult is responsible for that (45 ms). When your client submits a solved hash, it creates a SubmitResult and records the exact high-resolution nanosecond it left your computer using uv_hrtime(). When the pool replies "OK", done() is called, which subtracts the start time from the current time to calculate your network latency.

4. Storage.h: The C-to-C++ Bridge

This is a neat architectural necessity. The miner relies heavily on libuv (a C library) for networking. C doesn't understand C++ classes or objects.

When you tell libuv to open a socket, you have to give it a C-style callback function to run when data arrives. But how does that C function know which C++ Client object the data belongs to?

Storage acts as a global dictionary. It takes a pointer to your Client object, assigns it a simple integer ID (like 1), and gives that integer to libuv. When the libuv callback fires, it hands the integer back. The C++ code then looks up 1 in the Storage map, gets the Client pointer back, and seamlessly resumes object-oriented execution.

We have officially reached the end of the Network stack!

We have tracked your configuration from the terminal, through the Controller, into the Network router, packaged by the Client, and parsed into a Job.

The final step is to hand this Job to your CPU. Where to next?

The Core Manager (src/workers/Workers.cpp & Worker.cpp): How does it bind threads to your CPU hardware, manage CPU cache, and start the hashing loop?

The Cryptography (src/crypto/Lyra2.c & Algorithm.cpp): What is the actual mathematical blender that the blob gets thrown into?

<!DOCTYPE html>

.dashboard { display: grid; grid-template-columns: repeat(auto-fit, minmax(200px, 1fr)); gap: 10px; margin-bottom: 20px; }

.card label { display: block; color: #888; font-size: 0.8em; margin-bottom: 5px; }
